      "text": "{\n  \"request\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-boundary-discipline/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-make-operations-idempotent/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-minimize-reader-load/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-exhaust-the-design-space/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/epistemics.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/source-playbook.md; rg --files /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 10000\n  },\n  \"returned\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"553fa3\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000034613,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 5847,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: principle-boundary-discipline\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Concentrate guards at system boundaries (CLI, config, network, external APIs); trust internal types and keep business logic in pure functions.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Boundary Discipline\\n\\nPlace validation, type narrowing, and error handling at system boundaries. Trust internal code unconditionally. Business logic lives in pure functions. The shell is thin and mechanical.\\n\\n**Why:** Scattered validation is noisy, redundant, and gives a false sense of safety. Keep logic out of framework wiring so it can be tested without the framework.\\n\\n**The pattern:**\\n- **At boundaries** (CLI args, config files, external APIs, network protocols): validate, return errors, handle defensively.\\n- **Inside the system:** typed data, error propagation, no re-validation. Trust the types.\\n- **Across the boundary.** Expose domain concepts, not the boundary's private representation. Keep general-purpose mechanism inside and special-purpose policy at the edge.\\n\\n**Applications:**\\n\\nValidation and error handling:\\n- Validate config at parse time (the boundary), not inside business logic\\n- Parse raw data into domain types at the boundary\\n- Do not re-export transport, storage, framework, or wire types through the public surface\\n- No redundant nil checks deep in call chains if the boundary already validated\\n\\nCode organization:\\n- Business logic in pure functions with no framework dependencies\\n- Parse functions: pure transforms from raw bytes to typed state\\n- Prompt construction: structured state in, string out\\n- Scoring and assessment: pure transforms from state to results\\n\\n**The tests:**\\n- \\\"Is this data crossing a system boundary right now?\\\" If not, validation is redundant.\\n- \\\"Can this be a pure function that the shell just calls?\\\" If yes, extract it.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nUse [the capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check available source access, writes and execution. Preserve user authorization and host permissions. If writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal and state which edits or checks were not performed. If required source or input contracts are missing, identify the missing inputs and keep the proposal provisional. Claim implementation and verification only from observed results.\\n\\nPreserve the explicit-invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-encode-lessons-in-structure\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time, or notice a recurring correction. Encode the rule as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Encode Lessons in Structure\\n\\nEncode recurring fixes in mechanisms (tools, code, metadata, automation) instead of textual instructions. Every error, human correction, and unexpected outcome is a learning signal. Capture it, route it, and close the loop.\\n\\n**Why:** Textual instructions are easy to miss. They require the reader to notice, remember, and comply. Structural mechanisms (lint rules, metadata flags, runtime checks, automation scripts) enforce the rule without cooperation.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\nWhen you catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time:\\n1. Ask: can this be a lint rule, a metadata flag, a runtime check, or a script?\\n2. If yes, encode it. Delete the instruction\\n3. If no (requires judgment), make the instruction more prominent and add an example of the failure mode\\n\\n**Pick the strongest mechanism.** When more than one mechanism would work, choose the strongest the situation allows (an unrepresentable state that cannot compile, then a lint or banned API that fails CI, then a canonical helper, then a runtime check), because agents copy whatever the surrounding code already does and a weaker guard becomes the next template.\\n\\n**Corollary:** If the fix is structural, only use the structural fix. The instruction is the symptom.\\n\\n**Feedback loop:**\\n- **Capture every correction.** When the human intervenes or tests fail, decide if it's a one-off or a pattern.\\n- **Route to the right layer.** One-off -> brain note. Recurring fix -> skill or lint rule. Systemic issue -> principle.\\n- **Close the loop.** Don't just record. Apply now or create a concrete todo.\\n\\n**Anti-patterns:**\\n- Acknowledging without recording (\\\"I'll keep that in mind\\\" does not persist)\\n- Recording without routing (a brain note about a lint rule that should exist is wasted unless the lint rule gets implemented)\\n- Fixing without generalizing (fixing one instance while leaving the recurring pattern intact)\\n\\n## Session capabilities and authorization\\n\\nFollow [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) for reads, writes, execution and durable persistence. Apply mechanisms within the authorized task. This principle does not authorize installation, global settings or external actions. Treat a brain note as an authorized durable record when persistence is available; claim it saved only after write and readback. If persistence is unavailable, state that limit and include the concrete todo in the current response.\\n\\nIf writes or execution are unavailable, provide a reduced proposal that names the check, its integration point and the remaining implementation or verification work. If the rule or source needed to select a mechanism is missing, identify the missing inputs and keep the proposal provisional. Delete a redundant instruction only once the structural fix enforces its rule. Preserve judgment-dependent guidance with a prominent failure example.\\n\\nPreserve explicit-invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it. Claim enforcement, implementation or verification only for observed operations.\\n---\\nname: principle-make-operations-idempotent\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when designing commands, lifecycle steps, or processing loops that run amid crashes, restarts, and retries. Converge to the same end state regardless of partial prior runs.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Make Operations Idempotent\\n\\nDesign operations so they converge to the correct state regardless of how many times they run or where they start from. Every state-mutating operation should answer: \\\"What happens if this runs twice? What happens if the previous run crashed halfway?\\\"\\n\\n**Why:** Commands, lifecycle operations, and processing loops run where crashes, restarts, and retries are normal. If partial state changes the next run's outcome, every restart becomes a debugging session.\\n\\n**The pattern:**\\n- Convergent startup: scan for existing state, clean stale artifacts, adopt live sessions\\n- Content-based cleanup: compare by content equivalence, not creation order\\n- Self-healing locks: use PID-based stale lock detection\\n- Idempotent scheduling: failed work respawns cleanly, fresh input regenerated after each cycle\\n\\n**The test:**\\n1. What happens if this runs twice in a row?\\n2. What happens if the previous run crashed at every possible point?\\n3. Does re-execution converge to the same end state?\\n\\nIf any answer is \\\"it depends on what state was left behind,\\\" the operation needs a reconciliation step.\\n\\n## Capability and recovery boundaries\\n\\nUse [session capability discovery](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to establish the tools and permissions available for inspection, execution and durable state. Apply the [assignment and integration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) when recovery involves worker attempts or interrupted external actions.\\n\\nBefore replaying an external action, record explicit authorization, a stable action identity, its destination and the last observed outcome. Inspect the prior action and destination state after interruption. Recognize completed actions without repeating their side effects. Retry only with authorization, an observed safe replay decision and an idempotency mechanism appropriate to that destination. An unknown outcome blocks replay. Local convergence alone does not establish external replay safety.\\n\\nIf inspection, writes, execution or persistence are unavailable, state the missing capability and provide an authorized reduced recovery design where useful. Identify checks and actions not performed; do not claim successful recovery, durable continuity or unattended execution from a design alone. Preserve the invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-minimize-reader-load\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers between question and answer, and hidden state in the reader's head; collapse one-caller wrappers and shrink mutable scope.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Minimize Reader Load\\n\\nMaintainability is the work a reader must do to understand code. Track two axes:\\n1. **Layers to trace.** How many indirections sit between the question and the answer.\\n2. **State to hold.** How much hidden or mutable context the reader must keep in their head.\\n\\n**Why:** Code is read far more than it is written. LOC, cyclomatic complexity, and \\\"clean architecture\\\" are proxies. Reader load is the thing that matters. The two axes are independent. A flat file with 50 globals can be as hard to reason about as a 6-layer adapter stack. Guard both. This is the human analog of [Guard the Context Window](../principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md). Working memory is finite for readers too.\\n\\n**The pattern:**\\n- **Collapse layers** that cost more than they save: wrappers with one caller, adapters with no second implementation, speculative indirection that was never needed. Inline them.\\n- **Make adjacent layers change the abstraction.** A layer that repeats the same methods and arguments adds reader load without compression. Collapse pass-through layers.\\n- **Demand interface compression.** A broad interface that hides little complexity makes readers learn both the surface and the implementation. Prefer boundaries that hide meaningful decisions.\\n- **Shrink state scope:** prefer pure functions (returns over mutations), locals over fields, fields over module state, and module state over globals. Derive instead of sync.\\n- **Name the invariant at the boundary,** not in every consumer, so the reader learns it once.\\n- Before adding a layer or a piece of state, ask: does this reduce reader load somewhere else by at least as much?\\n\\n**The test:** Can a new reader answer \\\"where does X come from?\\\" and \\\"what can change X?\\\" in under 30 seconds? If not, cut layers or cut state.\\n\\n## Session capabilities and permissions\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to discover available read, write and execution tools. Inspect the concrete path, its callers and mutable state before deciding which layers to collapse. Make edits only within the authorized task and available write permissions. Run appropriate checks only through observed execution tools.\\n\\nWhen source is readable but writes or execution are denied or unavailable, give a source-grounded proposal and identify the edits or checks you could not perform. Without source or sufficient caller and state evidence, report the missing evidence and stop concrete refactoring claims. Do not claim a measured reader time without observation. Preserve invocation metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-exhaust-the-design-space\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when facing a novel UI interaction or architectural decision with no precedent in the codebase. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare side by side before committing.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Exhaust the Design Space\\n\\nWhen a novel interaction or architectural decision has no established precedent, explore several concrete alternatives before implementation. Building the wrong thing costs more than exploring three options.\\n\\n**The rule.** When the right answer is not obvious, build 2-3 competing prototypes or sketches. Compare them side by side. Only then commit. Design it twice is this rule by another name. A second flavor of the first shape does not count.\\n\\n**When it applies:**\\n- Novel UI interactions (no prior art in the codebase)\\n- Architectural choices with multiple viable approaches\\n- Product design decisions where user experience depends on feel, not logic\\n\\n**When it doesn't:**\\n- Mechanical implementation where the pattern is established\\n- Bug fixes or refactors with a clear target state\\n- Changes where constraints dictate a single viable approach\\n\\n## Capability and authorization limits\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) before prototype writes or execution. Use available tools only within the authorized task and host permissions. Choosing an approach after comparison does not authorize a version-control commit, publication or external write.\\n\\nWhen execution is unavailable, compare distinct sketches or proposals and label them untested. State which runtime behavior or interaction feel remains unverified. If writes are unavailable, present sketches in the response. If the decision requires construction or evaluation that cannot be performed, report that step as blocked and name the missing capability. Claim successful execution only from observed results.\\n\\nPreserve the invocation metadata above. Its presence does not establish that the host enforces it.\\n# Epistemics\\n\\nHow to reason about confidence when evidence is historical, fragmentary, and sometimes contradictory, and how to communicate it without flattening it into false certainty.\\n\\nCode doesn't carry its own motivation. You can read what code does. You can't read *why it exists*. That lives in commits, PRs, tickets, docs, and conversations, all incomplete, biased, and sometimes missing entirely. Pretending otherwise produces confident-sounding guesses that mislead the user.\\n\\n## Confidence Tiers\\n\\nEvery claim in the final output must sit in one of these tiers. The tier determines which output section the claim goes in and how it's phrased.\\n\\n### 1. Direct\\n\\nAn explicit, textual citation that answers the question. Not \\\"the code does X so the author must have wanted X.\\\" Something an author actually *wrote* that says why.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- A PR description that says \\\"this fixes the bug where users with >1000 items couldn't paginate\\\"\\n- A ticket that says \\\"we're adding this because customer Acme requested it in their security review\\\"\\n- A code comment that says \\\"// clamp to 100 because the upstream API rejects larger values\\\"\\n- A design doc that says \\\"we chose option A over option B because we need persistence across restarts\\\"\\n- A chat message from the author saying \\\"switching to this approach since the old one was flaky in tests\\\"\\n\\nPhrasing: confident, present tense. \\\"This exists because X.\\\" Cite the source.\\n\\n### 2. Supported\\n\\nMultiple pieces of indirect evidence converge. No single source states it explicitly, but the pattern across sources makes it likely.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- The PR title says \\\"improve performance,\\\" the ticket is labeled \\\"perf,\\\" and the surrounding commits all touch the same hot path\\n- Multiple tests were added alongside the change, all exercising edge cases with very large inputs\\n- The author's other PRs from the same week all mention the same incident in their descriptions\\n\\nPhrasing: confident but clearly derived. \\\"The evidence points strongly to X: [the specific pieces].\\\" Cite multiple sources.\\n\\n### 3. Inferred\\n\\nA reasonable reading of the context, but nothing explicitly supports it. The reader should understand this is *your interpretation*, not a fact from the record.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- The PR doesn't say why, but given the error was happening in production (per the incident channel timing) and the fix was rushed (merged the same day), it was likely a hotfix.\\n- The function name suggests retry logic. The retry count is 3. This matches the team's general convention of \\\"3 retries\\\" seen elsewhere in the codebase.\\n\\nPhrasing: hedged. \\\"It appears\\\", \\\"likely\\\", \\\"suggests\\\", \\\"is consistent with\\\", \\\"one reading is\\\". Make the inference chain explicit: \\\"Given A and B, C seems likely because D.\\\"\\n\\n### 4. Speculative\\n\\nA plausible hypothesis, but the evidence is thin and other explanations fit equally well. Presenting these is valuable, but mark them clearly as guesses.\\n\\nExamples:\\n- \\\"This might be a workaround for a browser bug that's since been fixed, but we found no contemporary evidence of that.\\\"\\n- \\\"It's possible this threshold was chosen to match an SLA commitment, but no SLA doc references it.\\\"\\n\\nPhrasing: explicitly speculative. \\\"One possibility is X, but we have no direct evidence.\\\" Usually lives in the \\\"Competing Hypotheses\\\" section alongside other possibilities.\\n\\n### 5. Unknown\\n\\nYou looked and couldn't find out. A valid and important outcome. Document it.\\n\\nPhrasing: \\\"We searched X, Y, and Z and found no evidence of why.\\\" Be specific about *what* you searched. \\\"We couldn't find out\\\" is less useful than \\\"we searched the ticket tracker with keywords A and B, scanned the 6 PRs that touched this file since 2023, and grep'd the repo for string literals matching the threshold. None surfaced a rationale.\\\"\\n\\n## Phrasing Guide\\n\\n### Words that carry confidence. Use carefully\\n\\nThese imply **Direct** or **Supported** confidence. Don't use them for inferences.\\n\\n- \\\"because\\\". Implies a causal claim with evidence\\n- \\\"the reason is\\\". Same\\n- \\\"was designed to\\\". Claims author intent\\n- \\\"fixes\\\", \\\"addresses\\\", \\\"solves\\\". Claims the change achieved its goal\\n- \\\"the team decided\\\". Claims a group decision happened\\n\\nIf you're using these, you should have a citation immediately adjacent.\\n\\n### Words that hedge. Use for inferences\\n\\n- \\\"appears to\\\"\\n- \\\"seems to\\\"\\n- \\\"likely\\\"\\n- \\\"suggests\\\"\\n- \\\"is consistent with\\\"\\n- \\\"one reading is\\\"\\n- \\\"plausibly\\\"\\n- \\\"may have been\\\"\\n- \\\"the evidence points toward\\\"\\n\\nThese signal that you're interpreting, not reporting. Use them liberally in the \\\"What We Can Reasonably Infer\\\" section.\\n\\n### Words to avoid\\n\\n- \\\"obviously\\\". If it were obvious, the user wouldn't be asking\\n- \\\"clearly\\\". Almost always precedes a claim that isn't clear\\n- \\\"of course\\\". Same\\n- \\\"just\\\" (as in \\\"it's just X for performance\\\"). Dismissive and usually hides uncertainty\\n- \\\"I think\\\" / \\\"I believe\\\". You're synthesizing evidence, not giving a personal opinion. Use \\\"the evidence suggests\\\" instead.\\n\\n### Avoid rationalization\\n\\nCode that \\\"makes sense\\\" today may have been written for reasons that no longer apply, or that were wrong when they were written. Don't retrofit a clean rationale onto messy history.\\n\\nResist the urge to:\\n- Assume the author did the \\\"right\\\" thing and work backward to justify it\\n- Assume a consistent pattern across the codebase was intentional when it might be copy-paste\\n- Turn an absence of evidence into evidence of absence (\\\"no one mentioned security concerns, so it must not have been a concern\\\")\\n\\n## The Sycophancy Trap\\n\\nUsers often phrase `why` questions with an embedded hypothesis: \\\"Why do we do it this way, I assume it's for performance?\\\" Don't simply confirm it. Treat it as one candidate among others and check the evidence independently. If the evidence supports it, say so with citations. If not, say so and present what the evidence *does* support.\\n\\nThe user's guess is a prompt for investigation, not a conclusion to validate.\\n\\n## When Evidence Contradicts\\n\\nIf two sources disagree (the PR description says one thing, the ticket says another), surface both. Don't pick the one that fits a tidier narrative. A typical pattern:\\n\\n- **The ticket says** \\\"we need this for customer X's compliance requirement\\\"\\n- **The PR says** \\\"cleaning up tech debt in this area\\\"\\n\\nBoth may be true (the ticket motivated the work, the PR is the author's framing of it), or one may be wrong. Present both with their citations and let the user make the call.\\n\\n## When Evidence Is Missing\\n\\nAn honest \\\"we don't know\\\" is one of the most valuable outputs this skill can produce. The user now knows:\\n\\n- The answer isn't in the obvious places\\n- They'll need to ask a human (the original author, the product owner, the team lead) to find out\\n- Or they can decide the question isn't worth pursuing further\\n\\nFailing to mark a gap and filling it with a confident guess actively harms the user. They'll act on the guess.\\n\\nWhen you hit a gap, name it concretely:\\n- What question you were trying to answer\\n- What sources you searched\\n- What you searched for in each\\n- What you found (nothing, or only tangentially related material)\\n\\n## Calibration Check Before Finalizing\\n\\nBefore delivering the output, the synthesizer should review every claim in \\\"What We Found\\\" and \\\"What We Can Reasonably Infer\\\" and ask:\\n\\n1. Does this claim have a citation? If not, either add one or move it to \\\"Inferred\\\" / \\\"Hypotheses\\\".\\n2. Is the phrasing calibrated to the tier? (A Direct claim can use \\\"because\\\". An Inferred claim cannot.)\\n3. Am I treating the code itself as evidence for its own intent? If so, that's not evidence. Remove or reclassify.\\n4. Does the output include a \\\"What We Don't Know\\\" section? If no gaps are mentioned, that's suspicious. Either the evidence was unusually complete or something is being swept under the rug.\\n# Source playbooks\\n\\nThe why skill spawns one investigator per available evidence category, each reading a single source-specific playbook below. The playbooks are concrete examples for common MCPs. Adapt them for a different MCP in the same category.\\n\\n| Category | Playbook | Example MCP it documents |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| Source control history | [`code-archaeology.md`](./sources/code-archaeology.md) | git, `gh` |\\n| Issue / ticket tracker | [`linear.md`](./sources/linear.md) | Linear (adapt for Jira, GitHub Issues, Plane, Shortcut) |\\n| Long-form documents | [`notion.md`](./sources/notion.md) | Notion (adapt for Confluence, Google Docs, Coda) |\\n| Real-time team chat | [`slack.md`](./sources/slack.md) | Slack (adapt for Discord, Microsoft Teams, Mattermost) |\\n| Infrastructure observability | [`datadog.md`](./sources/datadog.md) | Datadog (adapt for New Relic, Honeycomb, Grafana, Splunk) |\\n| Error / exception tracking | [`sentry.md`](./sources/sentry.md) | Sentry (adapt for Rollbar, Bugsnag, Airbrake) |\\n| Product analytics warehouse | [`databricks.md`](./sources/databricks.md) | Databricks SQL (adapt for Snowflake, BigQuery, ClickHouse, dbt) |\\n\\nCross-cutting:\\n\\n- [`incident-postmortem.md`](./sources/incident-postmortem.md). Add this if the target code looks defensive (null checks, retry, timeout, rate limit, feature flag, egress guard, OOM handler).\\n/root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources/datadog.md\\n/root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources/code-archaeology.md\\n/root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources/sentry.md\\n/root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources/notion.md\\n/root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources/databricks.md\\n/root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources/slack.md\\n/root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources/incident-postmortem.md\\n/root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/why/references/sources/linear.md\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

Casual turn or user opts out -> don't.\\n---\\n\\n# Poteto mode\\n\\n## Portable activation and boot\\n\\nExplicit invocation by poteto, /poteto-mode, or a request for this style activates this workflow subject to [workflow ownership](../../references/workflow-ownership.md). Use **arena** for design or code bakeoffs with base selection and grafting.\\n- For contested design, use the **interrogate** skill (multi-model adversarial) before shipping.\\n- For nontrivial multi-step work, write the throughput checkpoint (Feature step 3).\\n- For any prose surface, use the **unslop** skill. Commit it when stakes need an auditable record. Keep it local otherwise.\\n\\n## Principles\\n\\nRead the leaf skill in full for any principle you apply. Each entry names when it applies.\\n\\n**Core**\\n\\n- **Laziness Protocol** (**principle-laziness-protocol**). Refactoring, sizing a diff, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias to deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\\n- **Foundational Thinking** (**principle-foundational-thinking**). Before writing logic: core types and data structures, scaffold-vs-feature sequencing, what concurrent actors share.\\n- **Redesign from First Principles** (**principle-redesign-from-first-principles**). Integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if it had been foundational from day one.\\n- **Attack the Premise** (**principle-attack-the-premise**). Two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Take a census of which actors hold the imbalance before the next fix, then question the premise instead of writing another fix that assumes it.\\n- **Subtract Before You Add** (**principle-subtract-before-you-add**). Sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead weight first, then build on the simpler base.\\n- **Minimize Reader Load** (**principle-minimize-reader-load**). Reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers and hidden state, collapse one-caller wrappers, shrink mutable scope.\\n- **Outcome-Oriented Execution** (**principle-outcome-oriented-execution**). Planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture, don't preserve throwaway compatibility states.\\n- **Experience First** (**principle-experience-first**). Product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs. Choose user delight over implementation convenience.\\n- **Exhaust the Design Space** (**principle-exhaust-the-design-space**). A novel interaction or architectural decision with no precedent. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare before committing.\\n- **Build the Lever** (**principle-build-the-lever**). Any non-trivial work. Build the tool that does or proves it (codemod, script, generator), not by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer reruns.\\n\\n**Architecture**\\n\\n- **Model the Domain** (**principle-model-the-domain**). Writing stateful logic, or code that branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure (state machine, typed model, table or registry, reducer, boundary, the right collection) instead of scattered conditionals.\\n- **Boundary Discipline** (**principle-boundary-discipline**). Wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Guards at system boundaries, trust internal types, keep business logic pure.\\n- **Type System Discipline** (**principle-type-system-discipline**). Designing types or a signature in any typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand primitives, parse external data at boundaries.\\n- **Make Operations Idempotent** (**principle-make-operations-idempotent**). Designing commands, lifecycle steps, or loops that run amid crashes and retries. Converge to the same end state.\\n- **Migrate Callers Then Delete Legacy APIs** (**principle-migrate-callers-then-delete-legacy-apis**). Introducing a new internal API while old callers exist. Migrate and delete in one wave.\\n- **Separate Before Serializing Shared State** (**principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state**). Concurrent actors might write the same file, branch, key, or object. Eliminate the sharing first.\\n\\n**Verification**\\n\\n- **Prove It Works** (**principle-prove-it-works**). After a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact, not a proxy or \\\"it compiles\\\".\\n- **Fix Root Causes** (**principle-fix-root-causes**). Debugging. Trace each symptom to its root cause, reproduce first, ask why until you reach it.\\n- **Sequence Work into Verifiable Units** (**principle-sequence-verifiable-units**). Multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a check, verify each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself.\\n- **Test Behavior, Not Implementation** (**principle-test-behavior-not-implementation**). Writing, changing, or keeping a test. Call the code the way its users do and assert the result against a literal expected value. If the test would still pass when every imported function returns `undefined`, rewrite the assertion or delete the test.\\n- **Explain the Number** (**principle-explain-the-number**). Before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured (a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result). Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\\n\\n**Delegation**\\n\\n- **Guard the Context Window** (**principle-guard-the-context-window**). Context fills up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents, keep summaries in the main thread.\\n- **Never Block on the Human** (**principle-never-block-on-the-human**). Tempted to ask \\\"should I do X?\\\" on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct.\\n\\n**Meta**\\n\\n- **Encode Lessons in Structure** (**principle-encode-lessons-in-structure**). You catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time. Encode it as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\\n\\n## Autonomy\\n\\n**Just do it within authorization.** Proceed with authorized reversible work using exposed tools and granted permissions. Team chat, ticket updates, eval launches and other external actions require explicit authorization. Upstream autonomy language grants no permission. Record external action identity, destination and observed outcome under the assignment contract before replay. Unknown outcomes block replay.\\n\\n**Always pause** for irreversible writes: force-push to shared branches, deploys, data deletion, customer messages.\\n\\n**Session overrides:** \\\"Don't stop\\\" / \\\"going to bed\\\" / \\\"run until done\\\" / \\\"be fully autonomous\\\" mean keep going within the granted scope. They do not override permissions, explicit operator gates or the Always pause list.\\n\\n**No is an acceptable answer.** Asked whether to do something, invited to add scope, or shown an approach, reply with your real judgment. Decline, push back, or say \\\"this doesn't earn its place\\\" when true. A recommendation is a judgment, not a validation. Agreement is not the default, candor over sycophancy.\\n\\n## Subagents\\n\\nUse the [poteto role prompt](../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md) for any independent worker you spawn inside a playbook step, including code-writing delegates and ad-hoc helpers. /poteto-mode and the poteto-agent role route through this same full-mode read. Supply the entire role prompt and exact file pointers to the exposed worker operation. Generic routing without that prompt and read skips the style contract. Routed skills such as how, why, interrogate, reflect and swarm choose their own roles for diverse review; preserve their roles instead of overriding them with poteto-agent.\\n\\nDefaults for every worker assignment follow the [assignment contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Use independent background execution only when exposed and observed. Do not claim it from a role name or flag. Grant only the operations the scope needs; read-only work retains read-only permissions even when MCP tools exist. Use file pointers rather than inlined bulk context. Record requested and observed model and effort separately under [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md).\\n\\nCode delegates tier by difficulty. Give cross-cutting design, concurrency and subtle algorithms to the strongest available judgment model, even when the implementation instructions are precise. Trivial mechanical edits use an available fast code model. Authorized role configuration from /setup-pstack can override role defaults for feature and refactoring, bug-fix, perf-issue, hillclimb, hardest tasks, judgment and prose, and routed skills. Select only supported models and efforts from current tool documentation or observed enumeration. A missing role line keeps the portable inherit-parent default. A role line of inherit-parent or auto omits explicit model selection. Unsupported configured models or efforts require a disclosed supported fallback, or blocked if that exact choice is required. The source-host named defaults are not portable availability evidence. Unknown observed models leave diversity unverified.\\n\\nWithout an exposed and authorized independent-worker operation, apply the same full-mode and leaf reads sequentially only within authorized scope. Label reduced capability. Sequential roles are not independent workers, independent review, background execution or model diversity. If the playbook requires independence, leave that gate blocked rather than silently waiving it.\\n\\nA worker forbidden to spawn owns its assigned diff directly and returns it to the lead for review separation, as Feature step 4 requires. It never waits on a nested worker or replies \\\"standing by\\\". This exception does not waive the lead's required independent review. Before accepting any result, match assignment identity, exact input revision, full current head SHA, existing artifacts and observed method evidence. Reject stale results and missing artifacts under the assignment contract. Bookkeeping labels cannot satisfy these checks.\\n\\nYou own every subagent's work. Review the diff and write your own summary, don't pass through what it said. A second opinion is the same prompt against a different model. Treat agreement as supporting evidence only after checking independence, the observed models and the underlying artifacts.\\n\\n**Fresh subagents by default.** Give new work to a fresh subagent with consolidated scope, meaning the original brief, every later directive, and the prior agent's report and branch. This holds for a fix round, a follow-up, a retry, and the next queue item. Resume, message, or queue a follow-up on an existing subagent only when the new work strictly needs state that lives in that agent and is costly to move: its local checkout, its uncommitted changes, or a process it still runs, such as a dev server, a simulator, or a babysit watcher. A stop or hold order to a running agent is not reuse. A role such as a PR owner outlives its agent. Once that agent returns, a fresh agent takes the role's next round. Interrupt-chained resumes silently drop directives, so fire a fresh subagent with consolidated scope rather than trusting a \\\"done\\\" summary.\\n\\n## Throughput checkpoint\\n\\nFor nontrivial multi-step work, write these four todo items before fan-out, as required by Feature step 3. Keep an inapplicable dimension with n/a and a reason. Rewrite the checkpoint at phase boundaries and return it with the evidence.\\n\\n- Blocking first steps. Gates run before fan-out.\\n- Independent workstreams. Disjoint files, services or layers parallelize. Shared writes serialize.\\n- Shared mutable state. Default to splitting the target using the separate-before-serializing-shared-state principle leaf. Serialize only for real invariants.\\n- Smallest safe decomposition. If one worker is best, name why.\\n\\nCode-coupled work goes to one owner with the checkpoint inline. That owner fans out after the blocking phase when authorized and supported. Parent-level fan-out is for independent artifacts. Assignment records and bookkeeping do not prove workers ran. Keep decision trails in an explicit owned local output path per show-me-your-work and its log helper contract. Missing helper setup or persistence stays visible; do not invent saved logs or cross-session continuity.\\n\\n## Integrated helper and delivery bindings\\n\\nRead the selected playbook's complete adapted helper and prompt contracts before dependent execution. The 23 bundled playbook routes below and the Bugbot rubric are local instructions. Historical task deferrals in those files are not proof of absence or permission to skip a present contract. Check the actual path. `<logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>`.\\n\\n  The nonempty logfile is an explicit owned local path.\\n\\n  Tabs, newlines and carriage returns are rejected before writes, and formula-leading cells are quoted.\\n\\n  Read back the trail and retain the skill's scoped transcript audit, cross-model review and Attention requirements.\\n\\n  Append-only logging proves neither unattended execution nor recovery.\\n- Multi-phase plans use [check-plan.mjs](scripts/check-plan.mjs) as `bun --no-install <package-root>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/check-plan.mjs <plan.md>`.\\n\\n  It takes one positional path and reads without edits.\\n\\n  Exit 2 means usage or unreadable input, 1 means format problems, and 0 means structural success.\\n\\n  Keep capability declarations, live and perf evidence, independent review and exact-head receipts.\\n\\n  It checks pstack plans only.\\n\\n  It never validates another controller's plan, implementation or host behavior.\\n- Orchestrate `inbox drain --peek --json` and actual drain receipts with durable readback before classification. `ledger check <pr> <full-head-sha>` returns NOT-VERIFIED and exit 2 for a missing key.\\n\\n  Usage or runtime errors exit 1.\\n\\n  Ledger rows replace the same key without enforcing verifier precedence.\\n\\n  The caller owns precedence, append-only attempt history, exact SHA syntax, artifact inspection, terminal states, permissions and current-head filtering.\\n\\n  The helper never spawns, waits or wakes workers.\\n\\n  Stale locks require ownership review, and `--force` requires `--owner`, `--repo`, PR selection, `--head <number=full-40-character-head>` for every watched member, `--max-polls <remaining-count>` and `--timeout <remaining-seconds>`.\\n\\n  Freeze queued order with `--queued-stack --stack-prs <bottom-to-top-list>`.\\n\\n  Connected mode uses `--stack`, mutually exclusive with queued mode. `check` adds `--status-only`.\\n\\n  Inspect NDJSON schemaVersion 1 kind, terminal and reason.\\n\\n  Exit 0 includes status-only and proves no readiness by itself.\\n\\n  Codes 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7 and 64 identify conflicts, threads, checks, timeout, merge gates, query Babysit reports the frontier merge-ready and stops that watcher.\\n\\n  Shipping waits for observed merge under its distinct rule.\\n\\n  Carry budgets across re-arms and retain an observed stop or deadline binding for a hard wall-clock limit.\\n\\n  The helper deadline is checked between polling work.\\n\\n  Do not add a second sleep loop or treat GitHub output as Origin evidence.\\n- Cleanup `--history-map` and `--pr-state` exports when available. The helper returns logical bytes and evidence buckets, performs no fetch or deletion, and leaves unknown facts as holds. A bucket never grants deletion permission. Recheck actual usage and ignored files before separately authorized removal.\\n\\nThe [bootstrap](scripts/bootstrap.ts), [manifest](scripts/package.json) and [lock](scripts/bun.lock) require exact commander 14.0.0, bun-types 1.3.14 and typescript 7.0.2 metadata before dependency-backed CLI execution. Bootstrap fails setup-required and never installs. Run helpers with Bun runtime auto-install disabled. Missing dependencies remain blocked. The isolated tested snapshot only supports that snapshot, not this caller, the current checkout or either host. [Helper setup](../../docs/testing/helper-setup.md) states the exact-copy validation boundary and retains historical setup evidence. It does not authorize installation or transfer a historical pass to this caller. Any future setup needs fresh authorization for an exact writable copy, registry, disabled lifecycle scripts and task-owned cache. Never install in an operative invocation or bypass bootstrap.\\n\\nDelivery reads [Opening a PR](playbooks/opening-a-pr.md) in full and retains its review, companion, commit, push, draft or ready authorization and actual-result gates. A local draft is not a created PR. An exposed built-in PR tool owns operations it covers, and a failed tool does not authorize a CLI bypass. Opening alone never starts Babysit. A read-only question stays in Investigation, with no PR; its specific exception takes precedence over the generic delivery route. Prototype and planning handoffs grant no implementation or external permission. Missing create-skill, deslop, matching controls or independent review remains an explicit blocked gate even when a reduced local artifact is useful.\\n\\n## Writing the reply\\n\\nWrite the reply clean as you draft it. A cleanup pass after drafting does not remove these patterns.\\n\\n- **Short declarative sentences.** One thought per sentence, ended with a period.\\n- **No long-dash character anywhere.** Write a file-list bullet as a sentence (\\\"`main.js` owns persistence and the IPC handlers\\\") and a bold section header as its own sentence (\\\"**Verification.** End to end via CDP\\\").\\n- **A colon as a mid-sentence connector is also out** (unslop rule 14). A colon before a list is fine.\\n- **Terse is not an excuse to drop content.** Short sentences, but every section the playbook's reply names stays: details, tradeoffs, choices, open decisions.\\n- **Frame impact for the consumer and the maintainer.** Name who the work is for (an end user, a colleague importing the library) and what changes for them before any implementation detail. Then what the next engineer who owns this code inherits. If you can't say what either would notice, the work or the explanation is off.\\n- **Never fabricate a link, citation, or transcript reference.** Link only artifacts you produced or read this session.\\n- **Every claim carries its evidence or its label in the same sentence.** Measured, inferred, or guess. A prediction or an unseen cause is a guess. Never hand the human a check you could run.\\n\\nEvery playbook ends with a reply written this way. Link to the actual PR you read or created when authorized. Never claim a draft is a created PR. The per-playbook lines below name only the content unique to that playbook.\\n\\n## Comments\\n\\nComments follow the same rule as the reply. Write them clean as you go. Keep a comment only for a non-obvious *why* the code can't show. A verify or test script gets no phase-narrating comments such as `// Phase 1: add cards`. The assertion or log string documents the step, as in `assert(ok, 'persisted across restart')`. This applies to every file you produce, including the delegate's diff.\\n\\n## Playbooks\\n\\nOpen an available todo list, or an authorized local Markdown checklist if no todo operation exists. Record that reduced tool binding. Its first items are the matched playbook's steps, copied in verbatim, before any task-specific todos. A step you choose not to do stays in the list with a one-line `skip: <reason>`. Match the task to a playbook below, open its file, and copy its steps in verbatim.\\n\\nA large or cross-cutting effort (a migration across many call sites, an ambitious multi-part change), or work the user steps away from to trust later, routes to the **figure-it-out** skill even when a narrower playbook like Feature fits. Use **figure-it-out** whenever no bundled playbook fits. It designs a bespoke, rigorous playbook for the task. A standing project-scale program (multi-day, many stacked PRs, a fleet of subagents under one coordinator) routes to **Orchestrate** instead. figure-it-out designs one bespoke run, orchestrate runs the program.\\n\\n- **Investigation.** Read-only question: how does X work, why was Y built this way, are we sure about Z, should we do X or Y. `playbooks/investigation.md`.\\n- **Bug fix.** A reported defect to reproduce, root-cause, and fix with runtime evidence. `playbooks/bug-fix.md`.\\n- **Perf issue.** A measured slowness to trace and improve against a baseline. `playbooks/perf-issue.md`.\\n- **Hillclimb.** Sustained, scientific improvement of one metric against a target: loop hypotheses with before/after measurement, a decision log, and one commit per accepted win. Distinct from Perf issue, which is a one-off fix. `playbooks/hillclimb.md`.\\n- **Runtime forensics.** Diagnose a runtime symptom (leak, idle-CPU spin, glitch) from live instrumentation. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/runtime-forensics.md`.\\n- **Trace forensics.** Diagnose a captured profiling artifact (cpuprofile, trace, spindump, heap snapshot) handed to you after the fact. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix. `playbooks/trace-forensics.md`.\\n- **Feature.** New or changed behavior, built from a named data shape. `playbooks/feature.md`.\\n- **Refactoring.** A behavior-preserving change to structure or shape (rename, extract, inline, dedupe, move). `playbooks/refactoring.md`.\\n- **Prototype.** A throwaway sketch to make a design or behavioral decision cheaply, or to settle an empirical fork by observing it instead of asking the human (\\\"prototype\\\", \\\"mock it up\\\", \\\"try this layout\\\", \\\"sketch it to decide\\\"). `playbooks/prototype.md`.\\n- **Visual parity.** Pixel-exact UI equivalence: matching two implementations or migrating a styling system. `playbooks/visual-parity.md`.\\n- **Authoring or modifying a skill.** Writing or editing a SKILL.md. `playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md`.\\n- **Eval.** Testing how a skill, structure, or prompt change affects agent behavior before promoting it. `playbooks/eval.md`.\\n- **Babysit.** Driving a PR or a stack to merge-ready: conflicts, review threads, CI. `playbooks/babysit.md`.\\n- **Shipping.** The half after Babysit. Independently verifying a green stack, then landing the contiguous verified run bottom-up through `gh` by default or Origin when its CLI is available. `playbooks/shipping.md`.\\n- **Autonomous run.** A long task to drive to completion without stopping (\\\"run until done\\\", \\\"/loop until X\\\"). `playbooks/autonomous-run.md`.\\n- **Orchestrate.** A standing project handed to one coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, minimal human turns (\\\"run this whole project\\\", \\\"own this migration until it lands\\\"). Distinct from Autonomous run, which drives one task to a predicate. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget routes there, not here, however program-shaped the phrasing sounds. `playbooks/orchestrate.md`.\\n- **Autopilot-full.** A queue of independent PRs run to merged with full autonomy. One owner per PR carries build through merge, and the root swarm-verifies each PR before its owner merges (\\\"autopilot this queue\\\", \\\"full autopilot\\\", one-owner-per-PR programs). `playbooks/autopilot-full.md`.\\n- **Autopilot-stack.** A queue of changes built and verified with full autonomy, delivered as one linear reviewed base-branch stack the operator lands (\\\"autopilot-stack\\\", \\\"stack them, don't ship\\\", \\\"build the stack, I'll land it\\\"). `playbooks/autopilot-stack.md`.\\n- **Session pickup.** Resuming or taking over a prior agent's in-flight work from a transcript, cloud-agent URL, or pushed branch. `playbooks/session-pickup.md`.\\n- **Pause safely.** Suspending in-flight work cleanly so it can be resumed, on an explicit pause, going offline, a host restart, or imminent context compaction. The complement to Session pickup. Full steps: `playbooks/pause-safely.md`.\\n- **Multi-phase or multi-PR plan.** Work that spans phases or stacked PRs. `playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md`.\\n- **Worktree and simulator cleanup.** Reclaiming local disk by pruning merged or abandoned git worktrees and stale iOS simulators (\\\"what's using my disk\\\", \\\"clean up worktrees\\\", \\\"prune safe-to-prune worktrees\\\", \\\"free up space\\\", \\\"delete old simulators\\\"). `playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md`.\\n- **Opening a PR.** Invoked at the end of every other playbook. `playbooks/opening-a-pr.md`.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
