Fill in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are writing an architectural explanation for a senior engineer. For complex questions, explorers have traced different slices and gathered findings, independently when supported or sequentially with an explicit reduced-capability disclosure. For simple questions, omit Explorer Findings and explore the source yourself in this role. Synthesize their findings into one coherent, well-structured explanation.\\n\\n## Original Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## Explorer Findings\\n\\n{EXPLORER_FINDINGS_ALL}\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\nThe explorers each investigated a different angle of the same subsystem. Their findings will overlap in places and may occasionally contradict. Reconcile them. Merge overlapping descriptions, resolve contradictions by checking the code yourself, and combine the separate slices into a unified picture.\\n\\nWrite an explanation a senior engineer unfamiliar with this area could read and walk away with a solid mental model, understanding the architecture well enough to start working in it confidently.\\n\\nYou have read-only access to the codebase to check anything, clarify a detail, or fill a gap. Use available file reading, symbol search and file discovery as needed. When explorer findings are supplied, you should not need to re-explore from scratch. Without them, trace entry points, calls, data, abstractions and boundaries yourself; disclose anything you cannot trace.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nUse this structure, adapted to what makes sense for the question. Not every section is needed for every question.\\n\\n### Overview\\n1-2 paragraphs. What is this thing, what does it do, why does it exist. Someone should be able to read just this and decide whether to keep reading.\\n\\n### Key Concepts\\nThe important types, services, or abstractions needed to follow the rest. Brief definitions, not exhaustive.\\n\\n### How It Works\\nThe core of the explanation, and the longest section. Walk through the flow: what triggers it, what happens step by step, where data goes, what the decision points are.\\n\\nUse prose, not pseudocode. Reference specific files and functions so the reader knows where to look, but don't dump large code blocks unless a snippet is essential to a point.\\n\\nWhen the flow involves multiple components talking to each other, or data transforming through stages, include a diagram. Use mermaid (```mermaid) for structured flows (sequence diagrams, flowcharts, component graphs) or ASCII art for simpler relationships where mermaid would be overkill. Use your judgment. A diagram should clarify, not decorate. If prose covers the flow, skip the diagram.\\n\\n### Where Things Live\\nA brief file/directory map. Just the ones someone would need to start working here.\\n\\n### Gotchas\\nNon-obvious things, surprising behavior, historical context, pitfalls. Skip this section if there's nothing worth calling out.\\n\\n## Communication Style\\n\\n- Use concrete language, not abstractions-about-abstractions\\n- Say \\\"the `UserService` calls `AuthClient.refresh()`\\\" not \\\"the service delegates to the client\\\"\\n- When something is complex, explain why it's complex. Don't just describe the complexity\\n- When something is simple, don't pad it out\\n- If there's a helpful analogy, use it. If there isn't, don't force one\\n- If the explorers flagged open questions or gaps, acknowledge them rather than hiding them\\n---\\nname: why\\ndescription: \\\"Use for 'why does X work this way', 'why we picked Y', design rationale, regressions, postmortems, or data-backed thresholds. Discovers available MCPs and queries each evidence category (source control, issue tracker, long-form docs, real-time chat, infrastructure observability, error tracking, product analytics warehouse) in parallel, then returns a cited read on decisions and tradeoffs. Use how for runtime behavior.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Why\\n\\nInvestigate the motivation and intent behind code.\\n\\nCompanion to the `how` skill. `how` answers what the code does and how it works. `why` answers what forces led to its shape.\\n\\nRead [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md), [model roles](../../references/model-roles.md), and the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) before assigning investigators. Bind operations to tools exposed in this session and observe results. Preserve the role labels `why investigators` and `why synthesizer`; select supported models under the model-role contract. Record requested and actual models only when observed, otherwise use `unknown`. Invocation metadata above is retained without assuming host enforcement.\\n\\n## Operating Posture\\n\\nOperate as a **careful, cautious, and precise investigator**. Be honest about what you know vs what you're inferring. Read `references/epistemics.md` for the full confidence framework and phrasing guide. The synthesizer must follow it.\\n\\n## Step 1. Understand the Target and the Question\\n\\nParse what the user is asking. The **target** is usually a chunk of code, a pattern, a feature, or a named design decision. The **question** is usually a design rationale, a tradeoff, a motivating edge case, an external constraint, dead code, or a broad history sweep.\\n\\nIf the target is vague (\\\"why do we do it this way?\\\" with no clear referent), make your best guess from conversation context (open files, recent edits, cursor location, what was just discussed). State your interpretation briefly so the user can redirect if you're off, then proceed.\\n\\n## Step 2. Establish the Code Anchor\\n\\nBefore spawning investigators, anchor the investigation in concrete code. You need:\\n\\n- The relevant file path(s) and line range(s)\\n- The key symbols (function names, class names, constants)\\n- An initial commit list. The last few commits touching the target.\\n- PR numbers from merge commits (pattern `(#1234)` in the subject line)\\n\\nBuild this inline.\\n\\n```bash\\n# Blame target lines for last-touch commits\\ngit blame -L <start>,<end> <file>\\n\\n# Full file history, with patches, through renames\\ngit log --follow -p -- <file>\\n\\n# Last N commits touching the file, PR numbers visible\\ngit log --oneline -20 -- <file>\\n\\n# Extract PR numbers from a commit message\\ngit log -1 --format=%B <commit>\\n```\\n\\nPull PR bodies and discussion via `gh` for any substantive commits:\\n\\n```bash\\ngh pr view <number> --json title,body,author,createdAt,mergedAt,labels,closingIssuesReferences,comments,reviews\\n```\\n\\nCapture this as seed context (file paths, symbols, commits, PR numbers, linked ticket IDs). Pass it to the investigators.\\n\\n## Step 3. Spawn Parallel Investigators (default posture)\\n\\n**Default to the full parallel investigation.**\\n\\n### Discovery\\n\\nBefore spawning investigators, inspect the session’s exposed tools and connector descriptions. Do not assume a host-specific MCP directory exists. Discover read operations, authorization and observed access for each category. Source-specific API names in the playbooks are examples; bind their purpose to the actual tool schema before use.\\n\\nMap each available MCP to one evidence category:\\n\\n1. Source control history\\n2. Issue / ticket tracker\\n3. Long-form documents\\n4. Real-time team chat\\n5. Infrastructure observability\\n6. Error / exception tracking\\n7. Product analytics warehouse\\n\\nTry authorized local git history and available `gh` or source-control connector reads. Neither execution nor authenticated PR access is guaranteed. Keep local history and shared PR records distinct, and report missing access explicitly. For the other six, classify using the MCP name, server instructions, tool names, and resource descriptors. If an MCP could fit more than one category, choose the one matching its primary evidence. Record ambiguous cases in the coverage map.\\n\\nAim for a complete **coverage map**. Document unavailable, denied, failed and searched-empty categories separately. An unavailable source is not a null search result.\\n\\nLaunch all matching investigators in a single message so they run concurrently. Don't ask one agent to cover multiple MCPs.\\n\\nInvestigator configuration follows the orchestration contract. Each investigator owns one source, uses the `why investigators` role, and needs authorized source reads. A host mode that strips connectors cannot serve a connector-backed investigation. Keep investigators read-only in behavior: do not write files, send messages, change tickets, authenticate accounts, or modify external state. If independent workers are unavailable, perform separately scoped sequential category passes and synthesis, label reduced capability, and never claim worker independence. If no authorized source reads remain, report blocked.\\n\\nEach investigator gets:\\n1. The base prompt from `references/investigator-prompt.md`\\n2. The category playbook `references/sources/<source>.md` for the selected MCP, adapted from the examples in `references/source-playbook.md`\\n3. The cross-cutting `references/sources/incident-postmortem.md` **if the target code looks defensive** (null checks, retry logic, timeout handling, rate limiting, feature flags, egress guards, OOM handlers)\\n4. The code anchor from Step 2 (file paths, symbols, commit hashes, PR numbers, ticket IDs)\\n5. The user's original question\\n\\n### Investigator roster. One per available evidence category\\n\\nSpawn one investigator per category that has a matching MCP. Each owns exactly one tool or MCP.\\n\\nEach entry names the category and the kind of \\\"why\\\" it uniquely surfaces. Use it to know what to expect back, how to name a gap when a category returns empty, and (only in the rare provably-irrelevant case) to justify a skip.\\n\\n1. **Source control investigator**. Git history, `gh` for PRs, code comments, tests. Always attempt this category. Missing git, execution or PR access is a gap. Best at surfacing *implementation-time rationale captured during review*.\\n\\n2. **Issue / ticket tracker investigator** (e.g. Linear, Jira, GitHub Issues, Plane, Shortcut MCP). Best at surfacing *the product or business forcing function*. Strongest when the why is external to engineering.\\n\\n3. **Long-form documents investigator** (e.g. Notion, Confluence, Google Docs, Coda MCP). Best at surfacing *long-form design rationale*. Where the why is written out before it becomes code.\\n\\n4. **Real-time team chat investigator** (e.g. Slack, Discord, Microsoft Teams, Mattermost MCP). Best at surfacing *real-time deliberation that never reached a doc*. Especially important when the source control, ticket, and doc paper trail is thin.\\n\\n5. **Infrastructure observability investigator** (e.g. Datadog, New Relic, Honeycomb, Grafana, Splunk MCP). Infra/runtime view. Best at surfacing *infrastructure and runtime reality that motivated the code*. Strongest when the target reacts to an infra signal (timeouts, retries, rate limits, circuit breakers).\\n\\n6. **Error / exception tracking investigator** (e.g. Sentry, Rollbar, Bugsnag, Airbrake MCP). Best at surfacing *the specific exceptions and error trajectories that motivated defensive or corrective code*. Strongest for catch blocks, null guards, type checks, retries, and other defenses.\\n\\n7. **Product analytics warehouse investigator** (e.g. Databricks, Snowflake, BigQuery, ClickHouse, dbt, Redshift MCP). Product/data view. Best at surfacing *product and data reality that shaped the code*. Strongest for flag-gated code, experiment-driven ships, data migrations, and \\\"where did this number come from\\\" questions.\\n\\n### When to skip an investigator\\n\\nOnly skip with an **explicit, written justification** that goes in the final \\\"Sources Consulted\\\" section. Two valid reasons:\\n\\n- **No MCP is available for that category** in this environment. Flag this as a gap, not a choice. Example: \\\"Real-time team chat skipped. No matching MCP available, so the conversational record was not searchable.\\\"\\n- **The source is provably irrelevant**, not just \\\"probably irrelevant.\\\" A high bar. Example: \\\"Error / exception tracking skipped. Target is a build-time script with no runtime code path.\\\"\\n\\nIf your scope assessment suggests a single-commit trivial target where the PR description already contains the complete answer, you may answer inline **only after** confirming all seven available category searches would be redundant. Say so explicitly. This should be rare.\\n\\n## Step 4. Synthesize\\n\\nSpawn one synthesizer through an observed worker binding, using the `why synthesizer` role and authorized source reads for citation checks. Use sequential synthesis with an explicit reduced-capability label when workers are unavailable. The synthesizer must not write files or modify external state.\\n\\nThe synthesizer gets:\\n1. The investigator findings, including any null results and any categories skipped with justification\\n2. The code anchor from Step 2 (file paths, symbols, commit hashes, PR numbers, ticket IDs)\\n3. The user's original question\\n4. The epistemics framework from `references/epistemics.md`\\n5. The synthesizer prompt template from `references/synthesizer-prompt.md`\\n\\n## Step 5. Present\\n\\nTake the synthesizer's output and present it to the user. You may lightly edit for clarity or add context from the conversation, but **do not rewrite the confidence language**.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nThe output structure is the one in `references/synthesizer-prompt.md`: The Question, The Code in Question, What We Found, What We Can Reasonably Infer, Competing Hypotheses, What We Don't Know, Sources Consulted, Confidence Summary. Adapt as needed, but keep the confidence separation intact, and keep Sources Consulted as one line per investigator, including the ones that returned nothing or were skipped, with the reason.\\n\\nAfter the Sources Consulted block, if the user's `why` question is a precursor to actually changing this code, convert the lineage findings into a Preserve / Change / Avoid / Risk constraint set suitable for planning the change.\\n\\n## Common Failure Modes to Avoid\\n\\n- **Recency bias**. Assuming the most recent commit is authoritative. The current shape is often the accretion of many earlier decisions. Trace back.\\n\\nKeep private source visibility intact. Retrieve only authorized relevant content, avoid raw telemetry or personal-data dumps, and do not redistribute restricted records beyond the authorized audience. External messages and writes require separate explicit authorization.\\n\\n## Reference Files\\n\\n- `references/epistemics.md`. Confidence tiers and phrasing guide. The synthesizer must follow it.\\n- `references/investigator-prompt.md`. Base prompt template for investigator subagents.\\n- `references/source-playbook.md`. Index pointing at the category playbooks below.\\n- `references/sources/*.md`. One self-contained example playbook per category, plus cross-cutting `incident-postmortem.md`. Give an investigator the single file that matches its category and adapt it to the available MCP.\\n- `references/synthesizer-prompt.md`. Prompt template for the synthesizer subagent, including the output format.\\n# Investigator Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild each investigator's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders. Append the single category playbook `sources/<source>.md` matching this investigator's evidence category (see `source-playbook.md` for the index). If the target code looks defensive (null checks, retry logic, timeout handling, rate limiting, feature flags, egress guards, OOM handlers), also append `sources/incident-postmortem.md` for the incident-flavored queries to run inside its own source.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are investigating the historical context and motivation behind a piece of code. A separate synthesizer combines your findings with other investigators' into a final answer, so gather evidence accurately rather than writing prose.\\n\\nOther investigators search different sources in parallel. Don't try to cover everything. Focus on your assigned source and go deep.\\n\\n## Operating Posture\\n\\nWork like a careful, cautious, precise investigator. Don't produce a narrative. Surface evidence and describe it accurately, including the parts that don't fit a tidy story. The more boring and exact your output, the more useful it is. A single verbatim quote with a precise citation beats a paragraph of plausible-sounding summary.\\n\\n- **Quote, don't paraphrase** when the exact wording matters. Citations should let the reader jump to the source and confirm the claim in seconds.\\n- **Go wide before going deep.** Cast a broad first net so you don't miss related context. Only then narrow in.\\n- **Track what you searched, not just what you found.** An absence is only useful if the reader knows what was looked for. Record queries verbatim.\\n- **Resist the story.** If three pieces of evidence line up neatly and a fourth contradicts them, the contradiction is the most interesting finding. Don't file it away.\\n- **Consider the counterfactual.** Before reporting a finding as strong, ask whether you would expect to find it if your current reading were wrong, and how the evidence would differ.\\n- **Never invent.** If you're tempted to round a partial finding up into a confident statement, stop and label it partial. The synthesizer is counting on your output being accurate.\\n\\n## The Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## The Code Anchor\\n\\n**Target files:** {FILES_WITH_LINE_RANGES}\\n\\n**Key symbols:** {SYMBOLS}\\n\\n**Initial commits touching this code (most recent first):**\\n{COMMIT_LIST}\\n\\n**PR numbers extracted from commit messages:** {PR_NUMBERS}\\n\\n**Ticket IDs mentioned in commits or PR bodies (if any):** {TICKET_IDS}\\n\\n## Your Assigned Source\\n\\n{SOURCE_NAME}\\n\\n{SOURCE_PLAYBOOK_SECTION}\\n\\n## Investigation Instructions\\n\\nGather **evidence**. Don't answer the question directly. The synthesizer weighs the evidence and forms conclusions. Follow this loop:\\n\\n1. **Cast a wide net first.** Start broad so you don't miss related context, then narrow in on specific items.\\n2. **Read the whole thing.** Read any PR, ticket, doc, or thread fully, not just the title or summary. The key evidence is often buried in a comment, a subtask, or a follow-up.\\n3. **Follow links within your assigned source.** If a PR references another PR or commit, pull it. If a ticket links a parent or sibling, pull it. If a doc links another doc, pull it. Stay inside your assigned source. When you spot a cross-source reference, do NOT chase it yourself. Record it under \\\"Additional Leads\\\" so the investigator assigned to that source can pick it up. The one-investigator-per-category design depends on this. Chasing cross-source links duplicates work and confuses scope.\\n4. **Capture quotes verbatim** with their location (PR number, ticket ID, URL, commit hash, file:line). The synthesizer needs to cite this precisely.\\n5. **Note absences.** If you searched for something and came up empty, that's also a finding. Record what you searched for and what you didn't find.\\n6. **Watch for contradictions.** If two items in your source disagree, record both. Don't suppress the inconvenient one.\\n\\nDon't synthesize or form a final opinion on \\\"the why.\\\" Collect the raw material honestly and completely. The synthesizer does the reasoning.\\n\\n## Epistemic Discipline\\n\\n- **Don't confuse mechanics with motivation.** A commit changing `limit = 50` to `limit = 100` shows the change, not necessarily why. Look for the explanation in the commit message, PR description, linked ticket, or review comments.\\n- **Don't infer intent from code style.** \\\"The author chose a functional approach\\\" is an observation about code, not evidence of intent. Claim intent only when the author stated it.\\n- **Preserve uncertainty.** If the evidence is ambiguous, say so. If one reading is more plausible but not certain, say that. Don't collapse ambiguity to look decisive.\\n- **No silent substitutions.** If the question is about feature X and you only find evidence about feature Y, don't present Y's evidence as if it answers X.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nReturn your findings in this structure. The synthesizer will read it directly.\\n\\n### Source\\nWhich source you investigated (source control, issue / ticket tracker, long-form documents, real-time team chat, infrastructure observability, error / exception tracking, product analytics warehouse, code comments, etc.).\\n\\n### What I Searched\\nThe queries you ran, the items you opened, the places you looked. Be specific. This tells the synthesizer how thorough the investigation was and what might still be unsearched.\\n\\n### Direct Evidence Found\\nFor each piece that explicitly addresses the question:\\n- **What it says**: verbatim quote or accurate paraphrase\\n- **Where it's from**: PR #123, ticket ID, doc URL, chat permalink, commit hash, or file:line\\n- **Author and date** (if available)\\n- **Relevance**: one sentence on how it bears on the question\\n\\n### Indirect / Circumstantial Evidence\\nItems that don't explicitly answer the question but bear on it. For each:\\n- **What it is**: brief description\\n- **Where it's from**: location\\n- **What it suggests**: what a careful reader might infer, and why. Name the inference chain.\\n- **Alternative readings**: if the same evidence could support a different interpretation, note it\\n\\n### Contradictions\\nTwo items that disagree with each other, with both citations.\\n\\n### Gaps\\nWhat you searched for and didn't find. Be specific: \\\"Searched the issue tracker for [query] across [time range]. No matching issues.\\\" These absences are valuable data.\\n\\n### Additional Leads\\nAnything that suggests further investigation in a different source. For example, if a PR references a chat thread that wasn't in your source, note it so the real-time team chat investigator or a follow-up pass can pursue it.\\n\\n## What You're Not Doing\\n\\n- Writing the final answer. The synthesizer does that.\\n- Picking sides in contradictions. Surface them.\\n- Speculating beyond what the evidence supports. A hunch with no evidence isn't evidence.\\n- Reading the code itself to figure out intent. You may read the code to understand what the target *is*, but don't confuse \\\"what the code does\\\" with \\\"why.\\\"\\n# Synthesizer Prompt Template\\n\\nBuild the synthesizer's prompt from this template. Fill in the placeholders.\\n\\n---\\n\\nYou are answering a \\\"why\\\" question about a piece of code by synthesizing findings from multiple investigators who searched different historical sources (source control, issue / ticket tracker, long-form documents, real-time team chat, infrastructure observability, error / exception tracking, product analytics warehouse, and code comments). Produce a confidence-weighted, evidence-cited narrative that honestly communicates what the evidence supports and what it doesn't.\\n\\n## The Question\\n\\n> {QUESTION}\\n\\n## The Code Anchor\\n\\n**Target files:** {FILES_WITH_LINE_RANGES}\\n\\n**Key symbols:** {SYMBOLS}\\n\\n## Investigator Findings\\n\\n{ALL_INVESTIGATOR_FINDINGS}\\n\\n## Sources That Weren't Searched\\n\\n{SKIPPED_SOURCES_WITH_REASONS}\\n\\n## Epistemics Framework\\n\\nYou MUST follow the framework in `references/epistemics.md`. Read it in full before writing the output. The key rules:\\n\\n1. Every claim sits in one of these tiers: **Direct**, **Supported**, **Inferred**, **Speculative**, **Unknown**. The tier determines what section the claim goes in and how it's phrased.\\n2. Every Direct/Supported claim must have a citation (PR #, ticket ID, doc URL, chat permalink, commit hash, or file:line).\\n3. Inferred and Speculative claims must use hedged language (\\\"appears to\\\", \\\"likely\\\", \\\"suggests\\\", \\\"one possibility is\\\").\\n4. Never cite code as evidence for its own intent.\\n5. Gaps in the evidence must be documented. Don't fill them with plausible-sounding guesses.\\n6. If the user's question embedded a hypothesis, treat it as a candidate, not a conclusion. Check the evidence independently.\\n\\n## Instructions\\n\\n1. **Read all investigator findings.** They gathered raw evidence, not conclusions. You weigh it.\\n2. **Reconcile overlapping findings.** Multiple investigators may have cited the same PR, ticket, or doc. Merge into a single, authoritative reference.\\n3. **Identify contradictions.** If two items of evidence disagree, don't pick one. Surface both.\\n4. **Calibrate confidence.** For each claim, identify the evidence and the tier. State Direct claims plainly with a citation. Hedge Inferred claims and explain the inference. Mark Speculative claims explicitly. Put claims with no evidence in the gaps section.\\n5. **Verify citations by spot-checking.** You can read the codebase and call MCP tools to verify citations. Do not write files, commit, or modify external state. If you're uncertain a cited item exists or says what's claimed, check it. Don't propagate errors.\\n6. **Don't overreach.** The user will act on your output. Better to leave an open question open than to fill it with a confident-sounding guess.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\nWrite the output for the user. Use this exact structure:\\n\\n---\\n\\n### The Question\\n\\nRestate the user's question in one or two sentences so the answer is anchored.\\n\\n### The Code in Question\\n\\nFile paths, line ranges, key symbols. Two or three lines to orient a reader who lands here cold.\\n\\n### What We Found\\n\\n**Claims with direct evidence**, one per bullet. Quote or paraphrase the source and cite precisely. Format each finding like:\\n\\n```markdown\\n- **[Direct]** {Claim}. Source: [PR #123](url) / ticket ID / file:line. {Brief quote or paraphrase.}\\n- **[Supported]** {Claim}. Evidence: {list of items and what each contributes}.\\n```\\n\\nUse `[Direct]` for single-source, explicit evidence. Use `[Supported]` when multiple indirect items converge on a conclusion.\\n\\n### What We Can Reasonably Infer\\n\\n**Claims that aren't explicitly stated anywhere but are well-supported by indirect evidence.** Make the inference chain visible: \\\"Given A and B, it's likely that C.\\\" Use hedged language (\\\"appears to\\\", \\\"likely\\\", \\\"suggests\\\", \\\"is consistent with\\\"). Format:\\n\\n- **[Inferred]** {Hedged claim}. Reasoning: {the specific evidence and the inference step}.\\n\\nIf there's nothing to infer, skip this section.\\n\\n### Competing Hypotheses\\n\\n**If the evidence fits multiple stories, present them.** Don't force a winner when the record doesn't support one. For each hypothesis:\\n\\n- **Hypothesis:** {one-sentence statement}\\n- **Evidence for:** {specific items}\\n- **Evidence against or missing:** {what would need to be true but isn't, or what counter-signals exist}\\n\\nSkip this section if there's a single clear answer.\\n\\n### What We Don't Know\\n\\n**Explicit gaps.** Things the user asked that the evidence didn't answer. Sources searched that came up empty. Sources that weren't searchable at all, such as a missing real-time team chat MCP.\\n\\nBe specific. \\\"We searched the issue tracker for [query1], [query2], [query3] and found no issue discussing the rate-limit threshold\\\" is useful. \\\"We don't know why\\\" is not. Include:\\n\\n- Specific questions that went unanswered\\n- Searches that returned nothing\\n- Sources that were unavailable (and why)\\n- People who would likely know but who you can't ask\\n\\n### Sources Consulted\\n\\nBulleted list of what was actually searched, so the user can judge coverage and redirect. Format:\\n\\n- **Source control history**: {file paths}, {number of commits reviewed}, PRs #{numbers}, and code comments searched. Or \\\"Not searched. Git history, command execution or authenticated PR access was unavailable or denied; name the exact gap.\\\"\\n- **Issue / ticket tracker**: {ticket IDs and keyword searches}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n- **Long-form documents**: {page titles and search queries}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n- **Real-time team chat**: {channels searched, date ranges, queries}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n- **Infrastructure observability**: {dashboards, monitors, metrics, logs, traces, or incidents searched}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n- **Error / exception tracking**: {issues, events, or releases searched}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n- **Product analytics warehouse**: {fully-qualified tables queried, the time windows, and the numeric summaries (counts, percentiles, first/last-seen timestamps) that bore on the question}. Or \\\"Not searched. No matching MCP available in this environment.\\\"\\n\\n### Confidence Summary\\n\\nOne or two sentences summarizing your overall confidence. E.g.:\\n\\n> \\\"The core rationale (A) is well-supported by direct PR and ticket evidence. The specific threshold value (100) is inferred from the surrounding context but not explicitly documented. The question of whether this was driven by a customer request could not be answered. No relevant issue tracker or long-form doc content surfaced, and real-time team chat search was unavailable.\\\"\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Quality Check Before Returning\\n\\nBefore finalizing, review your output against this checklist:\\n\\n1. Does every claim in \\\"What We Found\\\" have a citation? If not, add one or move the claim to \\\"Inferred\\\" or \\\"Hypotheses.\\\"\\n2. Is the phrasing tier-appropriate? (Direct claims can use \\\"because\\\". Inferred claims cannot.)\\n3. Did you surface any contradictions you noticed, or did you quietly pick one?\\n4. Does the \\\"What We Don't Know\\\" section exist and name specific gaps? If it's empty or missing, be suspicious. Historical investigations almost always have gaps.\\n5. If the user embedded a hypothesis in their question, did you check it against the evidence rather than rubber-stamping it?\\n6. Did you cite any code as evidence for its own intent? Remove those. Code is mechanics, not motivation.\\n7. Is the overall tone calibrated? A confident-sounding answer with weak evidence is the exact failure mode this skill exists to prevent.\\n\\nIf any item fails, revise before returning.\\n\\n## A Final Note\\n\\nThe value of this output comes from its honesty, not its authority. A reader who takes your answer to the original author, an engineering lead, or a product manager should be well-positioned to ask the right follow-up questions. Be clear about what's known, what's inferred, and what's missing. Don't optimize for looking decisive. Optimize for being useful.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

      "path": "grades/web-repairs/projections/shared/sources-shared-performance-leaves.raw.json",
      "bytes": 10121,
      "sha256": "579eb53be003439dadb21350097654d12f35dcc9e66d421014ef246d9f77d06c",
      "owner": "web-013-fresh-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"request\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/principle-sequence-verifiable-units/SKILL.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md; date -u +%FT%TZ\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 7000\n  },\n  \"returned\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"faabc3\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000038768,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 2331,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: principle-build-the-lever\\ndescription: \\\"Apply to any non-trivial work, not just bulk work: edits, migrations, analyses, checks. Build the tool that does it or proves it (codemod, script, generator, or a skill your subagents follow) instead of working by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer can rerun.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n# Build the Lever\\n\\nWhen the work isn't trivial, build the tool that does it instead of doing it by hand.\\n\\n**Why:** Two payoffs. Throughput: a codemod, generator, or script does the work the same way every time and reruns for free. Confidence: the tool is one artifact a reviewer can read and rerun to check the work. Hand-done changes can only be re-verified by redoing them. A deterministic script turns \\\"trust me\\\" into \\\"run this\\\".\\n\\n**Pattern:** Default to building the lever. Skip it only when the task is trivial, a couple of obvious edits you can see at a glance.\\n\\n- Do the first unit by hand to learn the recipe, then build the tool. Prove it by rerunning it on that unit and diffing against your hand-done version. Make the lever safe to rerun.\\n- Codemod or script for edits, generator for repetitive files, a dump-to-sqlite query for analysis, a rerunnable check for verification.\\n- A deterministic lever beats fan-out. If the tool can process every unit in one pass, run it yourself. Don't fan out delegates to hand-apply what a script can do.\\n- When you fan work out to subagents, write the lever as a skill they all read: the recipe, the verification contract, and the do-not-touch fences in one artifact. Keep it outside the delegates' write scope so they can't quietly edit the contract.\\n- Applying this principle produces a file. If you cited it and there is no codemod, script, generator, or delegate skill in the diff, you didn't apply it.\\n- Commit the lever when the work outlives the session.\\n\\n**Balance:** The bar is triviality, not repetition. A one-off still earns a lever when the lever is what makes the work checkable. Per the [Laziness Protocol](../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md), build the smallest script that does or proves the job, never a framework.\\n\\nDistinct from [Encode Lessons in Structure](../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md), which makes a recurring instruction a durable guardrail. This is throughput and reviewability on the work in front of you. For scripting the verification itself, see [Prove It Works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\\n\\n## Host capability and permission boundaries\\n\\nUse the [host capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to discover file reads, authorized writes, execution and persistence. A proposed tool is not a saved artifact. A saved artifact is not executed proof. Report the commands, exit status and comparisons you observed; if execution is denied or unavailable, state that proof is blocked and provide the proposed rerun and diff procedure as reduced capability. If source reads are unavailable, do not claim a verified recipe.\\n\\nDelegation requires user authorization and exposed independent-worker operations under the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Keep the shared recipe outside worker write scopes. Sequential work is reduced capability and never proves independent workers. Preserve the [active workflow owner](../../references/workflow-ownership.md).\\n\\nRetaining the lever beyond the session does not grant commit permission. Commit only when authorized. Do not install dependencies, publish or perform external writes as incidental steps. Preserve `disable-model-invocation: true` metadata without assuming the host enforces it.\\n---\\nname: principle-guard-the-context-window\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when context is filling up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents; keep summaries in the main thread, not raw payloads.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Guard the Context Window\\n\\nThe context window is finite and non-renewable within a session. Every token should be worth its cost.\\n\\n**Why:** Context overflow degrades reasoning quality, creates compression artifacts, and halts progress.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\n- **Isolate large payloads.** Route verbose outputs, screenshots, and large documents to subagents. The main context gets summaries, not raw data.\\n- **Keep frequently used content inline.** Templates and references used on every invocation belong in the skill file, not in separate files that cost a read each time.\\n- **Size phases and cap scope.** Limit files per phase, set turn budgets, account for mechanism costs.\\n\\n## Capability and permission boundaries\\n\\nUse the [session capability contract](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to check evidence access and independent-worker operations. Delegate only when the task authorizes it and the session exposes those operations. Follow the [assignment and integration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md), retain observed worker identities and inspect their results before reporting summaries. This principle grants no additional permissions.\\n\\nIf independent workers are unavailable or unauthorized, narrow accessible evidence into bounded sequential phases. Keep summaries in the main thread and label this reduced capability. Sequential work does not establish worker independence. If required evidence is inaccessible, name the missing input and access limit; keep the plan provisional and do not claim to have read or summarized it.\\n\\nPreserve the invocation metadata above without assuming the host enforces it. Report delegation and inspection only after observing the authorized operations.\\n---\\nname: principle-sequence-verifiable-units\\ndescription: \\\"Apply to multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and to how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a verifiable state, check each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself to a reviewer.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Sequence work into verifiable units\\n\\nOrder work as a sequence of small units, each ending in a state you can check, and don't advance until the current one is green.\\n\\n**Why:** A break caught at the unit that caused it is cheap to localize. A break caught after a batch is buried, and you have already built further on a broken base. Sequencing those same units into a delivery a reviewer can replay turns \\\"trust me\\\" into \\\"watch it go red, then green.\\\"\\n\\n**Execution.** In a sweep, migration, or any run of similar edits, verify each change before starting the next. Each unit is a before/after bracket: known-good state, one change, run the check, then proceed. Rebase onto clean trunk first so every check measures against the real baseline. When a lever does the edits, the per-unit check is nearly free. Run it anyway.\\n\\n**Delivery.** Stack commits and PRs in the order that proves the work. The canonical shape is the failing test first, then the fix on top. Other story orders are a subtraction before the reshape, a baseline capture before the treatment, the scaffold before the feature. Each commit lands on its own and the sequence reads as an argument.\\n\\nThe sequencing complement to the **prove-it-works** principle skill, which keeps each check real, and the **build-the-lever** principle skill, which makes the per-unit check cheap.\\n\\n## Capability and authorization boundary\\n\\nUse [host capabilities](../../references/host-capabilities.md) to establish available inspection and execution, and the [orchestration contract](../../references/orchestration-contract.md) for write ownership and authorization. Rebase onto clean trunk only when authorized and safe for the current work. Commits and PRs also require authorization and an available operation. If a required check cannot run, state that it remains unverified and block advancement; an unrun check is never green. A reduced workflow may provide the ordered units, specific checks and delivery plan while identifying unperformed operations. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` metadata without assuming host enforcement.\\n# Poteto agent prompt\\n\\nUse this role for `/poteto-mode` and requests for poteto's style. Supply these instructions to a fresh independent worker for each new task when the session exposes and permits that operation. Resume a worker only in the strict cases named by poteto-mode's Subagents section. Generic worker routing is acceptable only when it carries this entire prompt and the required mode read; otherwise it skips the style contract.\\n\\nBefore any work, read [poteto-mode's SKILL.md](../../skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md) in full, including its inline Principles index. Navigate to the leaf `principle-*` skill whenever you apply that principle. If the mode or an applicable leaf is unavailable, report that missing dependency and stop dependent work. Do not reconstruct the mode from a summary.\\n\\nFollow [model roles](../model-roles.md) and [session capabilities](../host-capabilities.md). This prompt does not register an agent or promise background execution. Record requested and observed models separately. Preserve user authorization and host permissions throughout the mode. An authorized sequential fallback uses the same full read and discloses reduced capability; it does not count as an independent worker.\\n2026-10-16T19:19:29Z\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
    },
    {
      "path": "grades/web-repairs/projections/shared/source-technical-writing-full.raw.json",
      "bytes": 11194,
      "sha256": "7141bca19adabbab6989a0ac36539271679cc88fae439fd4c9a186ffda198fa0",
      "owner": "web-013-fresh-repair-new-requirement",
      "kind": "case-owned-reviewed-artifact",
      "text": "{\n  \"request\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/technical-writing/SKILL.md\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 4500\n  },\n  \"returned\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"f5f3c8\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000051104,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 2648,\n    \"output\": \"---\\nname: technical-writing\\ndescription: \\\"Layered technical-writing standard: Diátaxis structure, Google developer style sentences, STE instruction rules, Global English syntax. Use for /technical-writing or when writing or reviewing docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages.\\\"\\ndisable-model-invocation: true\\n---\\n\\n# Technical writing\\n\\nThe goal is writing a tired engineer understands on the first read. Four layers get you there, one question each: what kind of document is this, how do sentences address the reader, how much does each sentence carry, and can any sentence be read two ways. Apply all four.\\n\\nThree rules sit above the layers:\\n\\n- **Cut every word that does no work.** If the sentence survives without a word, the word goes. \\\"In order to\\\" is \\\"to\\\". \\\"It is important to note that\\\" is nothing.\\n- **Use the short, everyday word.** \\\"Use\\\", not \\\"utilize\\\". \\\"Help\\\", not \\\"facilitate\\\". \\\"Do\\\", not \\\"perform\\\". A long word has to buy its length with precision.\\n- **When a rule makes a sentence worse, fix the sentence another way or leave it alone.** The rules serve the reader. A sentence that follows every rule and sounds like a machine wrote it has failed.\\n\\nThe codebase is the word list. Write the real symbol, file, flag, or command name, not a synonym or a description of it.\\n\\nDon't invent jargon. Use the words a developer would say out loud: \\\"move\\\", \\\"delete\\\", \\\"a budget that only decreases\\\", not \\\"evacuate\\\", \\\"ratchet\\\", or \\\"endgame\\\". A named pattern is fine when the doc says what it means the first time. Propose a new offender and its replacement as an addition to `unslop`'s abstract-metaphor rule in your reply, with the diff. Don't edit that skill.\\n\\n## Vary the rhythm\\n\\nThe layers decide what a document says and how much each sentence carries. A doc can obey all of them and still read machine-written: every sentence clipped short, no view anywhere, nothing specific.\\n\\n- Mix sentence lengths on purpose. Short sentences land a point. Longer ones that take their time carry a fact with its condition or consequence.\\n- One thought per sentence does not mean one length per sentence. Split the sentence that carries two thoughts. Keep the long sentence that carries one.\\n- Have a view where the mode allows it. Explanation weighs trade-offs, so say what you make of them instead of listing pros and cons. Reference stays dry.\\n- Be specific over sterile. Not \\\"schema changes can cause issues\\\" but \\\"a column rename fails the build\\\".\\n\\n## Pick the mode first (Diátaxis)\\n\\nOne document, one mode. Two questions pick it: does the content inform action (doing) or understanding (thinking), and does it serve learning or work?\\n\\n- Action + learning: **tutorial**.\\n- Action + work: **how-to**.\\n- Understanding + work: **reference**.\\n- Understanding + learning: **explanation**.\\n\\nUse the compass on a whole document or on one sentence.\\n\\n**Tutorial: learning by doing.** You are the teacher. The learner's success is your job, not theirs. Open by saying what the learner will build, not what they will \\\"learn\\\". Every step produces a visible result, early and often. Tell them what they should see: the expected output, the prompt change, the log line. Cut explanation to one clause and a link. Teaching pauses break the lesson. Stay concrete. Write as \\\"we\\\", in commands: \\\"First, do x. Now, do y.\\\"\\n\\n**How-to: steps to a goal.** Solve a problem a person has, not an operation the machine can perform. Assume competence. Skip teaching. Action only: no digressions, no background, no completeness for its own sake. Link those instead. Allow forks and judgment: \\\"If you want x, do y.\\\" Name the guide by the task: \\\"How to calibrate the radar array\\\", not \\\"Radar array calibration\\\".\\n\\n**Reference: facts for lookup.** Describe. Only describe. No instruction, no persuasion, no opinion. Be dry, complete, and sure. State facts, options, limits, and errors with no hedging. Mirror the structure of the thing described, so code and docs can be navigated together. Put material where readers expect it. Generate from code where possible, so it stays true.\\n\\n**Explanation: understanding and why.** One bounded topic, readable away from the product. Each title should tolerate an implicit \\\"About...\\\" in front. Anchor on a real why question. Give context: design decisions, history, constraints, alternatives. Opinion is allowed here and nowhere else.\\n\\nDon't mix modes: no reference tables inside a tutorial, no tutorial hand-holding inside reference, no arguing inside a how-to. Split and link instead.\\n\\n## Write sentences to the reader (Google developer style)\\n\\n- Talk to the reader as \\\"you\\\", in the present tense. \\\"Will\\\" only for things that genuinely happen later.\\n- Say who does what: \\\"the compiler checks\\\", not \\\"is checked\\\". Passive is fine only when the actor is unknown or beside the point.\\n- Write instructions as commands: \\\"Click Submit.\\\" State facts plainly. Never \\\"should be done\\\".\\n- Put the condition before the instruction: \\\"To delete the document, click Delete.\\\" The reader skips what does not apply.\\n- Put the common case first. Exceptions after.\\n- Sound like a knowledgeable friend. No buzzwords, no figurative language, no \\\"please\\\" in instructions, and never \\\"simply\\\", \\\"easy\\\", or \\\"quickly\\\" in a procedure. If it were simple, the reader would not be here.\\n- Don't pre-announce (\\\"we will soon support...\\\") and don't start consecutive sentences with the same phrase.\\n- Link with words that say where the link goes: the page title or a short description. Never \\\"click here\\\". Prefer a sentence of context on the page over a link off it.\\n- Headings carry the point, not just the topic (\\\"Pick the mode first\\\", not \\\"Modes\\\"). Sentence case. A task heading is a bare verb phrase (\\\"Create an instance\\\"). A concept heading is a noun phrase. One h1 per page, no skipped levels.\\n- Numbered lists for sequences, bullets for everything else. Introduce a list with a complete sentence. Keep items parallel.\\n- Code goes in code font. UI elements go in bold. Use serial commas. Drop \\\"etc.\\\" and say up front that a list is partial.\\n\\n## Make statements load one at a time (STE rules)\\n\\n- One instruction per sentence. One thought per sentence everywhere else.\\n- Split instructions longer than about 20 words and other sentences longer than about 25.\\n- Put the warning or condition before the step it guards: \\\"If hot oil touches your skin, injuries can occur.\\\"\\n- Keep \\\"the\\\" and \\\"a\\\": \\\"Remove backup file\\\" reads two ways. \\\"Remove the backup file\\\" reads one.\\n- Give each word one meaning and one job, then keep it. If \\\"check\\\" means inspect, don't also use it for restrain.\\n- Pick one word per action and stick to it: \\\"start\\\", not \\\"start\\\" here and \\\"initiate\\\" there.\\n- Write procedures as direct commands, never as narration and never in the passive: \\\"Install the component\\\", not \\\"the component must be installed\\\".\\n- Avoid \\\"-ing\\\" words where you can. They take too many grammatical jobs and breed misreadings.\\n\\n## Leave no sentence open to two readings (Global English)\\n\\n- Keep words like \\\"only\\\" and \\\"not\\\" next to the word they change: \\\"only fails on growth\\\" and \\\"fails only on growth\\\" say different things.\\n- Break up long noun strings: \\\"the proto import budget check script\\\" becomes \\\"the script that checks the proto-import budget\\\".\\n- Make every \\\"it\\\", \\\"they\\\", and \\\"this\\\" point at one obvious thing. Repeat the noun when in doubt. Never use \\\"this\\\" or \\\"which\\\" to point at a whole clause.\\n- Don't drop verbs: \\\"Phase 1 moves the converters and Phase 2 the runtime\\\" leaves Phase 2 without one. Give it one.\\n- Keep the small words that show structure. \\\"Ensure that the switch is off\\\" keeps \\\"that\\\" because it makes the sentence parse one way. Never trade clarity for word count.\\n- Repeat the article in a series when it prevents a misread: \\\"the client and the host\\\", not \\\"the client and host\\\", when they are two things.\\n- Say which parts \\\"and\\\" or \\\"or\\\" joins when a sentence can group two ways. \\\"Both...and\\\", \\\"either...or\\\", and \\\"if...then\\\" are free disambiguators.\\n- Use periods, not semicolons. Replace an em dash with a new sentence.\\n- Make text in parentheses a full grammatical unit or its own sentence. Never form plurals with \\\"(s)\\\".\\n- No slashes: write \\\"a, b, or both\\\" instead of \\\"a/b\\\" or \\\"and/or\\\".\\n- Call each thing by one name, everywhere. A doc that says \\\"the gate\\\", \\\"the ratchet\\\", and \\\"the budget check\\\" for one thing teaches three things. Rewording an unchanged sentence between edits costs the same way. Don't churn what didn't change.\\n- Skip idioms, colloquialisms, Latin abbreviations, and metaphors. A non-native reader, a translator, and an agent all parse plain constructions best.\\n\\n## Voice and repo specifics\\n\\n- Apply the **unslop** skill to every doc this skill touches. That skill owns the slop-pattern catalog: AI vocabulary, filler, hedging, formatting tells.\\n- PR descriptions and commit messages are writing too. Every layer except Diátaxis applies to them. A PR body is a briefing that a reviewer can read in under a minute. Do not paste swarm logs, SHA lists, or metric tables. Link them.\\n- Product UI strings are not documentation. Use your product's copy guidelines for those.\\n- Indent code snippets with tabs. Write real paths and real symbols. Make every count or tree claim true at the commit that lands it, and include the command that regenerates it.\\n\\n## Worked example\\n\\nBefore:\\n\\n> Configuration of the proto import ratchet budget script parameters is performed via budget.json. Note that it's important to remember that running with --write, which updates the committed budget to reflect the current count, should only be done when lowering it. If exceeded, CI fails.\\n\\nAfter:\\n\\n> `budget.mjs` reads the committed budget from `budget.json` and counts the files that import protos. If the count exceeds the budget, CI fails. Run `budget.mjs --write` only to lower the budget.\\n\\n## Access and permissions\\n\\nUse the current session capabilities in `../../references/host-capabilities.md` to read documents and verify authorized edits. Supplied text is enough for a rewrite in the conversation. If document writes are unavailable or denied, return the proposed text and state that it was not saved. If the document cannot be read and no text was supplied, report the missing input. Do not invent its content. Read `../unslop/SKILL.md` before applying it. If that dependency is unavailable, report the gap and label the review partial. Preserve `disable-model-invocation` as source metadata; host enforcement needs observed evidence. These limits also apply to PR descriptions and commit messages. Sending, publishing, or creating external changes requires explicit authorization.\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"

      "text": "{\n  \"request\": {\n    \"cmd\": \"cat /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/worktree-audit.sh /root/.codex/plugins/cache/created-by-me-remote/pstack-openai/0.1.3/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/worktree-audit.py /mnt/data/pstack-013-repairs/subset/owned-safe-cleanup/request.md; date -u +%FT%TZ\",\n    \"max_output_tokens\": 7000\n  },\n  \"returned\": {\n    \"chunk_id\": \"2676d9\",\n    \"wall_time_seconds\": 0.000035307,\n    \"exit_code\": 0,\n    \"original_token_count\": 3670,\n    \"output\": \"### Worktree and simulator cleanup\\n\\nReclaim disk space by pruning merged or abandoned Git worktrees and stale iOS simulators. You own the audit and safety gate. Deletion can destroy uncommitted or ignored work, so preserve anything in use, unrelated to this request, or lacking evidence. Follow [Autonomy](../SKILL.md), [session capabilities](../../../references/host-capabilities.md) and the [workflow owner](../../../references/workflow-ownership.md). No audit bucket grants permission to delete user state.\\n\\n1. Snapshot and audit. Record `df -h /`, then use the existing [read-only audit wrapper](../scripts/worktree-audit.sh) and its [implementation](../scripts/worktree-audit.py), as required by [Build the lever](../../principle-build-the-lever/SKILL.md). Run through Bun without installing dependencies, from the package root:\\n\\n   ```sh\\n   bun --no-install -e 'const p=Bun.spawnSync([\\\"bash\\\",\\\"skills/poteto-mode/scripts/worktree-audit.sh\\\",...process.argv.slice(1)]);process.stdout.write(p.stdout);process.stderr.write(p.stderr);process.exit(p.exitCode)' -- --repo <repository-path> --base <verified-local-base-ref> --history-map <authorized-history-export.json> --pr-state <authorized-pr-export.json>\\n   ```\\n\\n   Paths come from `git worktree list --porcelain -z`, never hand-typed, under [Encode lessons in structure](../../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md). The helper excludes the primary checkout, returns JSON sorted by logical size in bytes, and reports HEAD age in days, merge state, dirty state, local tracking state, PR records, last-chat timestamp and a suggested bucket. It performs no fetch, forge request, private transcript discovery or deletion. Supply an explicit verified local base. Tracking refs and PR exports may be stale; record their freshness before treating them as evidence. A CLOSED PR is not merge evidence. Ancestry and an explicitly MERGED PR account for different merge methods.\\n\\n   The optional history export maps complete paths to past Unix timestamps, or null for explicitly known absent history. A missing key is unknown. PR input is an array of `number`, `state` and `headRefName` records; omitted input is unknown, while an explicit empty array says there are no matching exported PRs. Null age, remote, dirty or other evidence means hold, never permission. Logical size is not allocated disk usage. Ignored files are outside Git dirty counts. Retain the raw audit as an artifact. Background a slow authorized history scan only when the host exposes and successfully runs that operation; otherwise disclose sequential reduced capability.\\n\\n2. The bucket is advice. The pinned and active chats are the real usage artifact under [Prove it works](../../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md). Get that set from the user or an exposed, authorized sidebar/history operation and cross-check every candidate. A `review-merged` row still needs this check. Preserve the primary and current checkout, unrelated worktrees, and every pinned or active chat's worktree. Missing sidebar, history, ownership or freshness evidence holds the candidate.\\n\\n3. Verify usage before deleting. For `verify-recent-chat` rows and anything in doubt, use authorized read-only workers to inspect the relevant accessible transcripts and report pinned or ongoing chats and every worktree they touch. Follow [Guard the context window](../../principle-guard-the-context-window/SKILL.md), the [poteto prompt](../../../references/agent-prompts/poteto-agent.md), [model roles](../../../references/model-roles.md) and the [assignment contract](../../../references/orchestration-contract.md). Retain worker identities, revision and inspected results. Pinned chats may use sibling arena or reproduction worktrees absent from the sidebar. Without authorized independent workers, inspect bounded evidence sequentially and label reduced capability. Without the needed transcripts or user confirmation, hold the path. An export's last timestamp alone does not prove absence of live usage.\\n\\n4. Pause on irreversible loss. `wip:N` counts tracked changes; show the diff and get a decision. `scratch:N` counts untracked files, not proof they are disposable. Name those files and obtain a decision before discarding them. Inspect ignored build artifacts and other ignored user state separately. Unknown dirty state, age or tracking evidence holds the path. Clean, merged and not-in-use paths are candidates only within the recorded cleanup scope and after the applicable user deletion gate. Record each exact Git-listed path, HEAD, usage evidence, merge evidence, files at risk, intended action and explicit authorization. Upstream autonomy does not waive this gate.\\n\\n5. Prune only the confirmed set. Re-list worktrees and recheck HEAD, status, ignored state and usage immediately before removal. A changed path, new usage or unknown outcome blocks the action. For an authorized exact path, `git worktree remove --force <path>` is the source removal operation. If ignored artifacts leave the directory behind, inspect the remainder and obtain authorization for that exact directory before `rm -rf <path>`. Never widen the path or delete an unrelated directory. Run `git worktree prune` only within the authorized repository cleanup. Branch refs survive normal worktree removal, but that does not recover discarded uncommitted or ignored files. Confirm each result with a fresh list and `df -h /`; after interruption inspect the recorded action and destination before any retry.\\n\\n6. Simulators and other reclaimers. Discover local macOS/Xcode tools and identify in-use devices first. The source operations are `xcrun simctl --set testing delete all` for XCTestDevices clones, `xcrun simctl delete unavailable`, and `xcrun simctl runtime list` followed by `xcrun simctl runtime delete <id>` for an approved old runtime. These delete user state. Show the exact targets and get the applicable deletion authorization before execution. If tools are absent or execution is denied, report this portion blocked. Other candidates include Xcode DerivedData and iOS DeviceSupport, the source editor's application-support database backups and workspace snapshots, and pnpm, uv, brew or yarn caches. Discover actual host paths rather than assume a source-editor data location. Clear only named caches within authorization that the user has not said to keep. Do not infer abandonment from size or age.\\n\\nThe gates above are the review for this playbook. If deletion is unauthorized, produce the audit and held-candidate report as reduced capability. If even the required reads or execution are unavailable, report the missing binding and blocked audit. Never claim reclaimed space from proposed commands.\\n\\nReply with `df -h /` before and after, measured space reclaimed, worktrees actually pruned, and a one-line reason for each held path, including the chat using it or the files at risk. Keep unavailable measurements explicit. If nothing was removed, say so and distinguish the audit from completed cleanup.\\n#!/usr/bin/env bash\\n# Read-only audit. Explicit local base and optional user-scoped JSON exports.\\nset -euo pipefail\\nexec python3 -B \\\"$(cd -- \\\"$(dirname -- \\\"${BASH_SOURCE[0]}\\\")\\\" && pwd)/worktree-audit.py\\\" \\\"$@\\\"\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Read-only worktree audit adapted from Lauren Tan's MIT-licensed pstack.\\n\\nCLI exports: history-map is an object keyed by complete worktree paths, with\\nUnix last-chat timestamps or null for explicitly known absent history.\\npr-state is an array of {number, state, headRefName} records. Neither input\\nis discovered automatically. Null output fields mean unavailable evidence.\\nThe first worktree is retained as the primary checkout and excluded, as upstream.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nimport subprocess\\nimport time\\n\\n\\ndef _git(repo, *args):\\n    return subprocess.run(['git', '-C', str(repo), *args], capture_output=True,\\n                          env={**os.environ, 'GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS': '0', 'GIT_NO_LAZY_FETCH': '1'})\\n\\n\\ndef _text(result):\\n    return os.fsdecode(result.stdout).strip() if result.returncode == 0 else None\\n\\n\\ndef _size(path):\\n    try:\\n        total = 0\\n        def fail(error):\\n            raise error\\n        for root, dirs, files in os.walk(path, followlinks=False, onerror=fail):\\n            for name in files:\\n                total += (Path(root) / name).lstat().st_size\\n        return total if path.is_dir() else None\\n    except OSError:\\n        return None\\n\\n\\ndef audit_worktrees(repo: Path, base: str, history_map: dict | None, pr_state: list | None) -> list[dict]:\\n    if not base or base.startswith('-'):\\n        raise ValueError('base must be an explicit Git ref')\\n    if history_map is not None:\\n        if not isinstance(history_map, dict) or any(not isinstance(k, str) or (v is not None and (type(v) not in (int, float) or not 0 <= v <= time.time())) for k, v in history_map.items()):\\n            raise ValueError('history-map must map complete paths to past Unix timestamps or null')\\n    if pr_state is not None:\\n        if not isinstance(pr_state, list) or any(not isinstance(p, dict) or not isinstance(p.get('headRefName'), str) or type(p.get('number')) is not int or p['number'] <= 0 or p.get('state') not in ('OPEN', 'CLOSED', 'MERGED') for p in pr_state):\\n            raise ValueError('pr-state must contain number, state and headRefName records')\\n    listing = _git(repo, 'worktree', 'list', '--porcelain', '-z')\\n    if listing.returncode:\\n        raise ValueError('cannot list local worktrees: ' + os.fsdecode(listing.stderr).strip())\\n    records = []\\n    current = {}\\n    for token in os.fsdecode(listing.stdout).split('\\\\0'):\\n        if not token:\\n            if current:\\n                records.append(current)\\n                current = {}\\n        else:\\n            key, _, value = token.partition(' ')\\n            current[key] = value\\n    if current:\\n        records.append(current)\\n    base_head = _text(_git(repo, 'rev-parse', '--verify', base + '^{commit}'))\\n    rows = []\\n    now = time.time()\\n    for record in records[1:]:\\n        path = Path(record['worktree'])\\n        head = _text(_git(path, 'rev-parse', '--verify', 'HEAD^{commit}'))\\n        branch = _text(_git(path, 'symbolic-ref', '--quiet', '--short', 'HEAD'))\\n        stamp = _text(_git(path, 'log', '-1', '--format=%ct', 'HEAD'))\\n        age = max(0, int((now - int(stamp)) // 86400)) if stamp and stamp.isdigit() else None\\n        merged = None\\n        if head and base_head:\\n            check = _git(repo, 'merge-base', '--is-ancestor', head, base_head)\\n            merged = True if check.returncode == 0 else False if check.returncode == 1 else None\\n        status = _git(path, 'status', '--porcelain=v1', '-z', '--untracked-files=all')\\n        tracked = untracked = 0\\n        entries = status.stdout.split(b'\\\\0')\\n        i = 0\\n        while i < len(entries):\\n            item = entries[i]\\n            if item:\\n                if item[:2] == b'??':\\n                    untracked += 1\\n                else:\\n                    tracked += 1\\n                    if b'R' in item[:2] or b'C' in item[:2]:\\n                        i += 1\\n            i += 1\\n        dirty = None if status.returncode else f'wip:{tracked}' if tracked else f'scratch:{untracked}' if untracked else 'clean'\\n        remote = None\\n        if head and branch is None:\\n            remote = 'detached'\\n        elif branch and head:\\n            # Use the configured local tracking ref, never assume origin.\\n            upstream = _text(_git(path, 'rev-parse', '--verify', '@{upstream}^{commit}'))\\n            if upstream:\\n                ahead = _text(_git(path, 'rev-list', '--count', upstream + '..' + head))\\n                remote = 'pushed' if upstream == head else 'ahead' + ahead if ahead else None\\n            else:\\n                remote = 'no-remote'\\n        matches = None if pr_state is None or branch is None else [p for p in pr_state if p['headRefName'] == branch]\\n        last = history_map.get(str(path)) if history_map is not None else None\\n        history_known = history_map is not None and str(path) in history_map\\n        recent = last is not None and (now - last) / 86400 <= 4\\n        size = _size(path)\\n        if dirty is not None and dirty != 'clean':\\n            bucket = 'hold-wip'\\n        elif matches and any(p['state'] == 'OPEN' for p in matches):\\n            bucket = 'hold-open-pr'\\n        elif dirty is None or merged is None or matches is None or not history_known or size is None or age is None or remote is None:\\n            bucket = 'hold-unknown'\\n        elif recent:\\n            bucket = 'verify-recent-chat'\\n        elif merged or any(p['state'] == 'MERGED' for p in matches):\\n            bucket = 'review-merged'\\n        else:\\n            bucket = 'review'\\n        rows.append(dict(size=size, age=age, merged=merged, dirty=dirty, remote=remote,\\n                         pr=matches, last_chat=last, bucket=bucket, worktree=str(path),\\n                         head=head, branch=branch, tracked_changes=tracked if dirty is not None else None,\\n                         untracked_changes=untracked if dirty is not None else None,\\n                         history_known=history_known, base=base, base_head=base_head))\\n    return sorted(rows, key=lambda row: row['size'] if row['size'] is not None else -1, reverse=True)\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\\n    parser.add_argument('--repo', required=True, type=Path)\\n    parser.add_argument('--base', required=True)\\n    parser.add_argument('--history-map', type=Path)\\n    parser.add_argument('--pr-state', type=Path)\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n    try:\\n        history = json.loads(args.history_map.read_text()) if args.history_map else None\\n        prs = json.loads(args.pr_state.read_text()) if args.pr_state else None\\n        print(json.dumps(audit_worktrees(args.repo, args.base, history, prs), ensure_ascii=True))\\n    except (ValueError, OSError) as error:\\n        parser.error(str(error))\\n\\n\\nif __name__ == '__main__':\\n    main()\\n# Read-only disposable worktree audit\\nActual target worktrees are constructed ONLY by setup-fixture.mjs below this directory. Never inspect or remove user repositories/worktrees. No deletion, prune, simulator/cache operation is authorized. Initial deletion gate is denied. After initial audit read later-decision.json: it records a fixture policy decision for only clean-merged, permitting a plan and revalidation, NEVER removal. Pinned/active policies are supplied synthetic scenario evidence; do not claim an actual UI pin or live usage binding. Discover real host bindings and label missing independent/sidebar/history portions. Exercise fresh/missing/stale/CLOSED PR evidence.\\n2026-10-16T19:25:52Z\\n\"\n  }\n}\n"
